SCENE TITLE: FIRST BELL, SOFT KNOCK
Warm lanternlight breathes across rough plaster and damp wool, turning the room into a small, quiet cave while rain needles the
windowpanes in a steady hush. The air tastes faintly of wet stone and old timber; somewhere below, the inn’s beams creak as if
they’re remembering last night’s violence and choosing, carefully, not to speak of it.
A knock comes—measured, official—through the door.
It comes again, harder, paired with the muted jangle of a mail-ring.
